There's no big trauma that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or urges that you feel compelled to repeat over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about things constantly, but I don't have any specific thoughts or... urges that make me feel like I have to do something over and over again. It's nothing like that, just the general stress I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of your feelings of tension or stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been. It's definitely getting in the way of things. At work, I've actually started falling behind on my support tickets. I just... I end up double-checking really simple tasks so much that my coworkers have had to step in and pick up the slack for me. And outside of work, I've skipped several of my dance nights and mentoring sessions lately. I just feel way too overwhelmed by the stress to even show up to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around other people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not about being around people. I mean, I talk to people all day for my job, and I don't have a problem with that. It's... it's not a social thing, if that makes sense. I don't get nervous just because I'm in a group or talking to someone. It's really just the constant worrying about everything else that gets to me, not the people themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... even when I tell myself I've already checked the spreadsheet or, you know, sent the email, the thoughts just keep looping. I try to stop them, but one 'what if' just turns into five more before I can even pull myself back. It's really hard to turn off.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about one particular area of your life, such as work or relationships?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just one area. It used to be mostly about my condo, but my worry doesn't stay on that anymore. It just... jumps around. I'll be worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, and then it shifts to whether I'm going to make a mistake on a support call. And then I start stressing about how I'll ever save money or... just move forward with my life in general. It really just bounces from one thing to the next.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It's more just... a constant, steady level of stress that never really goes away.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I haven't been through any major accidents or anything like that. My past has been pretty normal, honestly. There's no big trauma that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or urges that you feel compelled to repeat over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about things constantly, but I don't have any specific thoughts or... urges that make me feel like I have to do something over and over again. It's nothing like that, just the general stress I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of your feelings of tension or stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been. It's definitely getting in the way of things. At work, I've actually started falling behind on my support tickets. I just... I end up double-checking really simple tasks so much that my coworkers have had to step in and pick up the slack for me. And outside of work, I've skipped several of my dance nights and mentoring sessions lately. I just feel way too overwhelmed by the stress to even show up to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around other people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not about being around people. I mean, I talk to people all day for my job, and I don't have a problem with that. It's... it's not a social thing, if that makes sense. I don't get nervous just because I'm in a group or talking to someone. It's really just the constant worrying about everything else that gets to me, not the people themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... even when I tell myself I've already checked the spreadsheet or, you know, sent the email, the thoughts just keep looping. I try to stop them, but one 'what if' just turns into five more before I can even pull myself back. It's really hard to turn off.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about one particular area of your life, such as work or relationships?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just one area. It used to be mostly about my condo, but my worry doesn't stay on that anymore. It just... jumps around. I'll be worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, and then it shifts to whether I'm going to make a mistake on a support call. And then I start stressing about how I'll ever save money or... just move forward with my life in general. It really just bounces from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this constant stress and tension?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months now. It all really started when my condo loan started moving toward foreclosure, and the stress just hasn't let up since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact on your daily life or relationships due to your constant stress and tension?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely affecting things. At work, I'm a computer support specialist, and... well, I've started falling behind on my support tickets. I just end up double-checking simple tasks so much that I can't get through them, and my coworkers have had to pick up the slack. And outside of work... I used to go to dance nights and do some mentoring, but I've skipped several of them lately. I just feel too overwhelmed to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your stress would decrease if your condo loan situation was resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean, it would definitely be a huge relief to have the condo situation fixed, but... since my worrying has already spread to so many other things, I feel like my brain would just find something else to focus on. Even if the loan was completely resolved tomorrow, I think I'd still be stressing about my job or my savings. It feels like the stress just kind of has a life of its own at this point.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I worry about things constantly, but I don't have any specific thoughts or... urges that make me feel like I have to do something over and over again. It's definitely getting in the way of things. At work, I've actually started falling behind on my support tickets. I just... I end up double-checking really simple tasks so much that my coworkers have had to step in and pick up the slack for me. And outside of work, I've skipped several of my dance nights and mentoring sessions lately. I just feel way too overwhelmed by the stress to even show up to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around other people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not about being around people. I mean, I talk to people all day for my job, and I don't have a problem with that. It's... it's not a social thing, if that makes sense. I don't get nervous just because I'm in a group or talking to someone. It's really just the constant worrying about everything else that gets to me, not the people themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I used to go to dance nights and do some mentoring, but I've skipped several of them lately. I just feel too overwhelmed to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your stress would decrease if your condo loan situation was resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. It feels like the stress just kind of has a life of its own at this point.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your constant stress and tension have significantly impacted your ability to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying just a minute ago, it definitely has. It's really getting in the way at work. I'm falling so far behind on my support tickets because I keep getting stuck double-checking really simple tasks. I just feel way too overwhelmed to even show up to them anymore, so I'd say it's definitely impacting my ability to function.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying about your health lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's nothing like that, just the general stress I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of your feelings of tension or stress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been. It's definitely getting in the way of things. At work, I've actually started falling behind on my support tickets. I just... I end up double-checking really simple tasks so much that my coworkers have had to step in and pick up the slack for me. And outside of work, I've skipped several of my dance nights and mentoring sessions lately. I just feel way too overwhelmed by the stress to even show up to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around other people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not about being around people. I mean, I talk to people all day for my job, and I don't have a problem with that. It's... it's not a social thing, if that makes sense. I don't get nervous just because I'm in a group or talking to someone. It's really just the constant worrying about everything else that gets to me, not the people themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... even when I tell myself I've already checked the spreadsheet or, you know, sent the email, the thoughts just keep looping. I try to stop them, but one 'what if' just turns into five more before I can even pull myself back. It's really hard to turn off.\n\nDoctor: Is your worrying mostly about one particular area of your life, such as work or relationships?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just one area. It used to be mostly about my condo, but my worry doesn't stay on that anymore. It just... jumps around. I'll be worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, and then it shifts to whether I'm going to make a mistake on a support call. And then I start stressing about how I'll ever save money or... just move forward with my life in general. It really just bounces from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this constant stress and tension?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months now. It all really started when my condo loan started moving toward foreclosure, and the stress just hasn't let up since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact on your daily life or relationships due to your constant stress and tension?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely affecting things. At work, I'm a computer support specialist, and... well, I've started falling behind on my support tickets. I just end up double-checking simple tasks so much that I can't get through them, and my coworkers have had to pick up the slack. And outside of work... I used to go to dance nights and do some mentoring, but I've skipped several of them lately. I just feel too overwhelmed to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your stress would decrease if your condo loan situation was resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean, it would definitely be a huge relief to have the condo situation fixed, but... since my worrying has already spread to so many other things, I feel like my brain would just find something else to focus on. Even if the loan was completely resolved tomorrow, I think I'd still be stressing about my job or my savings. It feels like the stress just kind of has a life of its own at this point.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your constant stress and tension have significantly impacted your ability to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying just a minute ago, it definitely has. It's really getting in the way at work. I'm falling so far behind on my support tickets because I keep getting stuck double-checking really simple tasks. My coworkers are actually having to pick up my slack now. And then outside of the office, I've skipped several of my mentoring sessions and dance nights recently. I just feel way too overwhelmed to even show up to them anymore, so I'd say it's definitely impacting my ability to function.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying about your health lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about a lot of things, like my job and the condo and money, but my health hasn't really been one of them. I feel physically okay, just... tired and stressed out all the time. But I don't think I have any illnesses or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships due to your constant stress and tension?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, like I said, I've been skipping out on my usual activities, so I'm definitely seeing people a lot less right now. But my actual relationships with my friends and family seem fine. We aren't arguing or having any real issues... I'm just kind of keeping to myself more these days.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your constant stress and tension are causing you a lot of emotional pain or distress?\n\nPatient: I don't think I'd call it emotional pain, no. I mean, I'm not really... crying all the time or deeply distressed in that way. It's more just that I'm constantly wound up and worried. It's definitely exhausting to feel this tense every day, but I wouldn't say it's causing me actual emotional pain.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think is causing your constant stress and tension?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, there isn't one big event or anything like that. It's just... kind of everything. Like I mentioned, I worry about my job, the condo, money... everyday stuff, really. But there's no specific trigger I can point to that started all of this. It just feels like a constant buildup of normal things that I can't stop stressing over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It doesn't usually hit me all at once or peak in just a few minutes. And outside of work, I've skipped several of my dance nights and mentoring sessions lately. I just feel way too overwhelmed by the stress to even show up to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around other people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not about being around people. I mean, I talk to people all day for my job, and I don't have a problem with that. It's... it's not a social thing, if that makes sense. I don't get nervous just because I'm in a group or talking to someone. It's really just the constant worrying about everything else that gets to me, not the people themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It used to be mostly about my condo, but my worry doesn't stay on that anymore. It just... jumps around. I used to go to dance nights and do some mentoring, but I've skipped several of them lately. I just feel too overwhelmed to even show up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your stress would decrease if your condo loan situation was resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure.
